This paper introduces a five-arm ablation methodology to disentangle the effects of retrieval-warmed energy-based reasoning (RW-EBR) on structured reasoning tasks, showing a 35 percentage point balanced accuracy improvement on graph reachability. This work provides a systematic diagnostic framework for understanding which components of warm-started diffusion samplers drive performance gains, which is crucial for improving iterative reasoning in structured tasks. The five arms are oracle, best-constant, per-query-random, shuffled, and aligned, adapted from LLM-RAG evaluation. On connectivity-2, the aligned vs. shuffled oracle swing reaches +35 pp balanced accuracy, isolating per-graph alignment as the dominant factor.

## [Evaluation-Strategy Gap in DL Fault Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26492) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study reveals a 0.190 balanced accuracy gap between within-program cross-validation and whole-program holdout in fault diagnosis of deep learning programs, attributed to program-level feature structure. This finding highlights a critical methodological flaw in current fault diagnosis evaluation, potentially leading to overestimated performance on unseen programs and hindering reliable AI deployment. The study uses DynFault, a corpus of 5,542 fault-injected training traces from 38 real-world DL programs, and identifies that curvature features are useful for instability detection on unseen programs, while optimizer and activation features only help on seen programs.

## [DiARC Boosts LLM Reasoning on ARC via Preference Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26530) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose DiARC, a method that improves large language model reasoning on Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus (ARC) tasks by constructing preference pairs from positive and negative samples and applying preference alignment. The approach introduces three ways to generate negative samples: output-level visual transformations, DSL-level rule inversion, and task-specific rule editing. This work addresses the high cost of closed-source models and the poor performance of open-source models on ARC-like reasoning tasks. By incorporating negative samples via preference alignment, DiARC offers a cost-effective way to enhance LLM reasoning, potentially advancing AI's fluid intelligence capabilities. DiARC constructs preference pairs where the positive sample is the correct output and the negative sample is a plausible but incorrect alternative, keeping the observed demonstrations unchanged. Experimental results across multiple ARC-like benchmarks show consistent performance improvements over baseline models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: The Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus (ARC) consists of grid-based puzzle tasks designed to measure fluid intelligence in AI systems. These tasks are easy for humans but challenging for AI, typically providing only a few input-output examples. Preference alignment is a technique used to fine-tune language models to prefer certain outputs over others, often via methods like RLHF or DPO.

VoiceTTA introduces a reinforcement learning-based test-time adaptation method that improves zero-shot text-to-speech imitation of unseen speaking styles without requiring fine-tuning. It optimizes learnable prefixes via group relative preference optimization in a flow matching-based model at inference time. This work addresses a critical limitation of zero-shot TTS systems—failure to imitate uncommon speaking styles like crosstalk or dialects—enabling rapid personalization without large datasets. It opens new possibilities for adaptive speech synthesis in real-world applications. VoiceTTA uses two style rewards based on coefficient-of-variation differences of F0 and energy, combined with speaker similarity and intelligibility (WER from Whisper). It employs group relative preference optimization (GRPO) to update learnable prefixes in a flow matching-based TTS model at inference time.

Flow matching is a generative modeling technique used in speech synthesis for high-quality audio generation.

This method significantly reduces the cost of deploying large language models by enabling efficient ternary quantization with minimal performance loss, potentially making LLMs more accessible for edge devices and resource-constrained environments. The paper introduces the Optimal Transport Flow Concept Bottleneck Model (OTF-CBM), which replaces static projection with dynamic cross-modal transport to align visual and textual concepts. It uses Inverse Optimal Transport to learn a semantic cost and unbalanced optimal transport for flow matching. OTF-CBM addresses a key limitation of existing Concept Bottleneck Models by enabling fine-grained, geometrically meaningful concept alignment, improving both classification accuracy and interpretability. This advances the field of interpretable AI for vision-language tasks. The model employs velocity-based concept activation, avoiding ODE integration while capturing interpretable geometric relations. Experiments demonstrate superior classification accuracy and concept faithfulness compared to prior CBMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) are interpretable architectures that predict through an intermediate layer of human-understandable concepts. However, existing vision-language CBMs often rely on pre-aligned encoders or global cosine similarity, which fails to capture fine-grained concept localization. Optimal transport provides a principled way to measure distances between distributions, and Inverse Optimal Transport infers cost functions from observed transport plans.

## [Robust-TO: Confidence-Aware Tool Orchestration for Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26904) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Robust-TO, an agentic video understanding framework that addresses the blind trust problem by integrating per-frame reliability scores into every reasoning stage, achieving 56.4% accuracy on clean inputs and 54.3% under corruptions, outperforming Gemini-2.5-Pro and open-source baselines. This work tackles a critical vulnerability in video reasoning models—blind trust in corrupted frames—which can cause 15-30% accuracy drops in real-world applications like autonomous driving and surveillance. Robust-TO's approach could make AI video understanding more reliable for safety-critical deployments. Robust-TO uses a unified evidence interface where each tool returns a prediction, temporal grounding, and a calibrated reliability score. It employs a three-tier synthesis process (high/medium/low confidence) and a confidence-cost GRPO reward to jointly optimize correctness, reliability, and efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Video reasoning language models often assume all input frames are equally reliable, leading to the blind trust problem where models fail to detect degraded visual evidence. Robust-TO is an agentic framework that orchestrates heterogeneous tools (e.g., object detection, motion tracking) and selects trustworthy frames based on reliability-relevance scores before reasoning.

## [Risk-Aware Selective Driver Monitoring Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26922) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a cost-aware selective inference framework for multimodal driver monitoring that uses a lightweight RGB-physiological student model and a learned gate to balance fast predictions with safety interventions, reducing unsafe false negatives from 17.37% to about 5%. This work addresses a critical latency-reliability trade-off in automated vehicle driver monitoring, potentially improving safety in Level 3+ autonomous driving by enabling real-time, risk-aware decisions without always relying on large, slow vision-language models. The RGB-physiological student model achieves 0.7440 Macro-F1 and 0.9099 balanced accuracy with only 11.39M parameters and 3.08ms inference latency. The framework also includes a driver-state world modeling module that predicts future errors and action costs, though worst-group evaluations show persistent calibration drift.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Driver monitoring systems in automated vehicles need low-latency inference to quickly detect driver state changes, but large vision-language models are too slow and unreliable for always-on use. Selective inference frameworks allow a model to abstain from making a prediction when uncertain, triggering a safety intervention instead. Learned gating mechanisms adaptively decide when to accept a fast prediction or defer to a more cautious action.

This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating and improving multi-reference image generation, which is essential for applications like personalized content creation and design, and demonstrates that dynamic reward optimization can effectively handle complex combinations of reference images. ## [XMSE-Aware Adaptive Empirical Bayes Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26975) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes an XMSE-aware mixed estimator that adaptively combines maximum likelihood and empirical Bayes shrinkage, with theoretical guarantees on oracle regret and risk. This work addresses a known limitation of empirical Bayes methods—poor performance under kernel misspecification—by providing a principled way to retreat toward maximum likelihood when needed, potentially improving robustness in statistical estimation. The estimator uses a closed-form oracle mixing weight derived from a scalar quadratic fixed-weight XMSE, and a plug-in implementation with finite-sample XMSE approximations achieves second-order oracle regret. Simulations on Silverbox and Cascaded Tanks benchmarks show it retains regularization benefits when helpful and retreats toward ML under misspecification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Empirical Bayes (EB) methods estimate prior distributions from data and shrink estimates toward a common mean, often improving over maximum likelihood (ML) in terms of mean squared error. However, recent excess mean squared error (XMSE) analysis revealed that kernel-based EB can be worse than ML when the kernel is poorly aligned with the true parameter. This paper turns that diagnostic into a design principle by creating an adaptive estimator that interpolates between ML and EB.

## [ICMPG: LLM Planning Meets Physics for Realistic Motion Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26981) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose In-Context Model Predictive Generation (ICMPG), a framework that integrates large language model planning with physics-aware model predictive control to generate physically realistic human motions from open-vocabulary text descriptions. This work bridges the gap between semantic fidelity and physical realism in text-driven motion synthesis, enabling more versatile and controllable generation for applications like animation, gaming, and robotics. ICMPG consists of two modules: Context-Aware Motion Generation (CAMG) uses an LLM as a planner to decompose text commands and generate candidate motion sequences, while Model Predictive Generation (MPG) evaluates candidates via physical simulation and semantic alignment in a closed-loop refinement process.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Existing text-to-motion methods face a trade-off: LLM-based approaches handle diverse instructions but often violate physics, while physics-aware models ensure realism but struggle with semantic complexity. Model Predictive Control (MPC) is a control technique that optimizes actions over a receding horizon using a model of the system. ICMPG reformulates motion synthesis as an MPC-like process to combine the strengths of both paradigms.

This work addresses key limitations of prior OpenRE approaches—clustering lacks label generation and LLM-based methods struggle with discriminative capacity—by introducing structured reasoning, improving generalization to unseen relations. A new study systematically compares absolute, relative, and forecast state representations for a Double DQN agent in pumped-storage energy trading, finding that combining all three feature families achieves 55.6% on the test set and 47.5% cross-zone median, far outperforming absolute features alone (28.8% and 5.7%). This work demonstrates that state representation is a central design choice in reinforcement learning for energy trading, not a minor preprocessing step, with significant implications for robust policy transfer across different electricity markets. The study uses the HydroDam pumped-storage arbitrage environment with a fixed Double DQN agent, training on 2007–2011 Belgian day-ahead prices and evaluating on 2012–2025 same-market data and 39 other ENTSO-E zones. Combining absolute, relative, and forecast features yields the best performance, while absolute features alone perform poorly.

## [CIRCLE: Data-Free Reservoir Features for Cold-Start Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27095) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose CIRCLE, a class-incremental learning method that uses fixed bidirectional reservoir features and streaming linear discriminant analysis (SLDA) heads, never training the backbone on image data. CIRCLE addresses the computational tension between drift-compensation and frozen-backbone methods, enabling efficient cold-start continual learning with strong performance on long task horizons (up to 500 splits) while training much faster. CIRCLE groups multiple random reservoir instantiations into feature ensembles and averages softmax outputs of independent SLDA heads, achieving a tunable bias-variance tradeoff. On CIFAR-100, TinyImageNet, ImageNet-Subset, and ImageNet-1k, it outperforms strong baselines at 50, 100, and 500 task splits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Class-incremental learning (CIL) requires a model to learn new classes sequentially without forgetting previous ones. Cold-start exemplar-free CIL is a challenging setting where no replay data or external pretraining is allowed. Existing methods either train the backbone throughout the stream (drift-compensation) or freeze it after the first task (frozen-backbone), both with limitations. Reservoir computing uses a fixed, untrained dynamical system to map inputs into high-dimensional spaces, and streaming LDA allows closed-form updates without storing past data.

## [Study Reveals Breakdowns in AI Healthcare Chatbots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27302) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A large-scale study analyzed over 15,000 user reviews from 59 AI healthcare chatbot apps, identifying three recurring breakdowns: access barriers and service unreliability, user experience and interaction quality, and billing and customer support issues, with privacy and security concerns linked to the most negative experiences. This study frames AI healthcare chatbots as information infrastructures, highlighting how failures in access, usability, and trust affect users, and offers actionable insights for designers, policymakers, and information professionals to improve digital health systems. The researchers used topic modeling and interpretive analysis on user reviews to identify the breakdowns, and they found that privacy and security concerns are associated with the most negative experiences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Information infrastructure theory views systems like chatbots as interconnected socio-technical networks that enable information flow. Topic modeling is a machine learning technique that automatically identifies themes in large text collections. This study applies both to understand user-reported issues in AI healthcare chatbots.

## [PEEU: Boosting GUI Agents via Experience Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PEEU, a method that enables GUI agents to autonomously explore environments and use hindsight experience to synthesize high-level training data, improving task planning and cross-website generalization. A 7B model using PEEU achieves 30.6% accuracy, outperforming the much larger Qwen2.5-VL-32B model. This work addresses a critical limitation of small open-source multimodal LLMs in GUI task planning, offering a cost-effective and privacy-preserving alternative to large commercial models. The proposed TDHAF framework also provides a systematic way to analyze compositional generalization, which is valuable for future research. PEEU autonomously explores environments to discover planning experiences and uses hindsight learning to synthesize aligned training data. The TDHAF framework analyzes compositional generalization across three task granularities (low, middle, high) and reveals that high-level task training yields stronger out-of-distribution generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal web agents (GUI agents) assist humans with repetitive GUI tasks by decomposing complex tasks into executable actions. Small open-source multimodal LLMs are cost-efficient and privacy-preserving but often suffer from weak planning and limited cross-website generalization compared to large commercial models. PEEU aims to bridge this gap by enabling autonomous experience exploration and utilization.

SciFig is an end-to-end multi-agent framework that generates editable, high-quality methodology figures from scientific text, decomposing the process into planning, layout synthesis, component rendering, and iterative refinement. This addresses a key pain point in scientific communication by balancing editability and visual quality, potentially saving researchers significant time and effort in figure creation while enabling easy revision as papers evolve. SciFig produces XML figures editable in standard diagramming tools, generates figures in about 10 minutes on average, and outperforms seven baselines across all four axes of the proposed SciFig-Eval protocol.

SciFig uses a multi-agent system with specialized agents for planning, layout, rendering, and refinement, leveraging VLMs for iterative feedback to improve quality.

## [MoDE: Tuning LLMs via Late-Layer Ensemble](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13077) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce Mixture-of-Depths Ensemble (MoDE), a tuning framework that treats late layers of a transformer as an ensemble with learned routing weights to improve reasoning performance. MoDE can be applied on top of existing methods like LoRA and shows modest gains with small parameter overhead. This work provides a simple, parameter-efficient way to leverage intermediate layer representations for better LLM reasoning, potentially reducing the need for larger models or more complex tuning. It builds on interpretability insights from the logit lens, making late-layer predictions directly useful for training. MoDE adds learned routing weights to combine logits from late layers into the final prediction, with only a small number of additional parameters. Experiments show that MoDE modestly improves reasoning performance compared to standard fine-tuning or LoRA alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer-based LLMs typically use only the final layer's loss for fine-tuning and final-layer representations for predictions. However, interpretability tools like the logit lens reveal that late-layer representations already contain largely formed predictions. MoDE exploits this by treating late layers as an ensemble, allowing their predictive power to be harnessed during training.

Researchers propose ReFine, a two-component framework that combines symbolic if-then rules with dual-granularity filtering to generate high-quality synthetic tabular data when reference data is scarce. This addresses a critical bottleneck in domain-specific machine learning where limited data often hinders model performance, offering a 7.48% average improvement in extreme low-data regimes. ReFine extracts symbolic rules from interpretable models to guide LLM-based generation and applies dual-granularity filtering to reduce localized redundancy while preserving rare samples.

Prompt-based LLMs offer flexibility but often produce distributionally drifted data with localized redundancy. ReFine overcomes these issues by combining symbolic rules (explicit if-then logic) with dual-granularity filtering (coarse and fine levels) to better match the target distribution.

## [AI Ethics Research Needs Process Transparency, Not Just Output Checks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08639) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper argues that AI-assisted ethics research requires process transparency grounded in agent-integrity, rather than relying solely on output evaluation or reproducibility. The authors propose a documentation-adequacy framework with five transparency elements, demonstrated by the paper itself. This work addresses a critical gap in current AI disclosure mandates, which require reporting AI assistance but fail to specify what transparency serves. By linking transparency to agent-integrity, it offers a nuanced perspective that could reshape how AI-assisted research is documented and evaluated in philosophy and beyond. The paper argues that ethical inquiry is essentially contested at two levels—what it is and what it demands of the inquirer—defeating output-only evaluation and reproducibility framings. The proposed framework operationalizes Meaningful Human Control through five elements: declaration, navigation, documentation account, process documentation, and development records.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Current AI disclosure mandates in scholarship require reporting AI assistance but leave transparency philosophically unspecified. The concept of agent-integrity, drawn from moral philosophy, refers to the coherence of an agent's identity-constituting commitments. This paper applies that concept to argue that transparency in AI-assisted research should enable tracking of authorial commitments, not just evaluation against settled criteria.

Patent documents have inherent sections (abstract, claims, description) that are semantically related, providing natural positive pairs.

This work improves recommendation quality for both head and long-tail items, which is crucial for real-world platforms where most items have sparse interactions. ## [Residual RL-MPC Boosts Microrobotic Cell Pushing Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05448) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a hybrid controller combining model predictive control (MPC) with a residual reinforcement learning policy trained by soft actor-critic (SAC) for microrobotic cell pushing under time-varying Poiseuille flow. The residual policy outputs a bounded 2D velocity correction that is applied only during robot-cell contact, improving tracking accuracy and robustness over pure MPC and PID in simulation. This work addresses a key challenge in contact-rich micromanipulation under microfluidic flow, where small disturbances can break contact. The hybrid approach offers a practical way to combine the reliability of MPC with the adaptability of RL, potentially advancing automated cell manipulation for biomedical applications. The residual policy is contact-gated, meaning corrections are only applied during robot-cell contact, preserving reliable approach behavior. A residual-bound sweep identified an intermediate correction limit as the best trade-off, and the controller generalized from a clover training curve to unseen circle and square trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Microrobotic cell pushing in microfluidic environments is challenging due to time-varying flow disturbances that can break contact and cause drift. Model predictive control (MPC) is a common approach for trajectory tracking but may lack robustness to unmodeled dynamics. Reinforcement learning (RL), particularly soft actor-critic (SAC), can learn adaptive policies but may be unreliable during approach phases. The proposed hybrid method combines both to leverage their strengths.

## [Recoverability Maps for License Plate Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces recoverability maps, a task-agnostic method that quantifies the boundary of reliable license plate recovery from degraded urban imagery under extreme viewing angles. This work addresses a key challenge in opportunistic urban sensing by determining when AI-based restoration can reliably recover license plates, with implications for surveillance, traffic monitoring, and smart city applications. The method combines a dense synthetic sweep of degradation parameters with two summary measures: boundary area-under-curve and a reliability score. The best restoration model (among U-Net, Restormer, Pix2Pix, and SR3 diffusion) recovers about 93% of the parameter space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Urban environments contain many imaging sensors (e.g., CCTV, dashcams) that can be repurposed for license plate recognition under the opportunistic sensing paradigm. Recent AI-based restoration can recover information from degraded images, but it is unclear which distortion parameters allow reliable recovery.

## [IFSC: Fair Strategic Classification with Peer Imitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Individual Fairness-aware Strategic Classification (IFSC), a framework that models peer-driven manipulation where agents imitate nearby positively decided peers to obtain favorable outcomes, and learns classifiers under post-manipulation distributions. This work addresses a critical gap in strategic classification by considering interdependent manipulation under individual fairness constraints, which is more realistic for many real-world settings like loan approvals or college admissions where applicants may imitate successful peers. IFSC employs a robust learning process that introduces stochastic perturbations during manipulation simulation to account for uncertainty in peer observability. Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets show that IFSC improves individual-fairness consistency and mitigates imitation-induced distortions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Strategic classification studies how agents manipulate their features to get favorable decisions from a predictive model. Existing fairness-aware approaches typically assume agents act independently and focus on group fairness, but individual fairness requires that similar individuals receive similar outcomes, which can lead to peer imitation and interdependent manipulation.

## [SymQNet: Amortized RL for Low-Latency Adaptive Hamiltonian Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12808) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SymQNet introduces an amortized reinforcement learning approach that reduces acquisition latency in adaptive Hamiltonian learning by up to 72.6x on Ising benchmarks compared to traditional Bayesian methods. This breakthrough makes adaptive Hamiltonian learning practical for repeated low-latency workloads, which is crucial for real-time calibration and characterization of quantum devices. At five qubits, SymQNet reduces acquisition-only decision latency by 47.1x and 72.6x relative to bounded Fisher-information search and bounded two-step BALD; at twelve qubits, full simulated steps take 1.02 seconds versus 13.27 seconds for bounded two-step BALD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Adaptive Hamiltonian learning is a process used to calibrate quantum devices by iteratively selecting experiments based on previous outcomes. Traditional methods recompute Bayesian design rules after each posterior update, which can take seconds per step, accumulating significant wall-clock time over many shots. Amortized reinforcement learning trains a policy offline to make fast decisions online, avoiding repeated expensive computations.

## [Trust in GenAI Health Info: Dependency Hurts Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20605) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Two randomized experiments with 901 participants found that while accurate GenAI health information increases trust, learned dependency makes users more likely to trust incorrect outputs, and text highlighting does not reduce overreliance. This study highlights a critical risk in using GenAI for health information: users who become dependent on AI may fail to calibrate trust appropriately, increasing susceptibility to misinformation. It underscores the need for interface designs that promote critical evaluation rather than passive reliance. The experiments involved 338 college students and 563 Amazon Mechanical Turk workers, using a 2x2 between-subjects design with accuracy (correct vs. incorrect) and highlighting (highlight vs. no highlight) as factors. The interaction between accuracy and dependency was significant (p < 0.001), indicating that highly dependent users trusted incorrect information more.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Trust calibration refers to the alignment between a user's trust in an AI system and the system's actual reliability. Learned dependency occurs when users become over-reliant on AI tools, potentially reducing their critical thinking. Generative AI (GenAI) models like GPT-4 can produce convincing but incorrect health information, making trust calibration especially important.

## [NBGL: Noise-Aware Generative Learning for Ultrasound Speckle Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25009) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a Noise-Aware Boundary-Enhanced Generative Learning (NBGL) framework that simultaneously reduces speckle noise and preserves anatomical boundaries in ultrasound images, adapting to varying noise levels. This work addresses key limitations of existing speckle reduction methods—over-smoothing boundaries and poor generalization to heterogeneous noise—potentially improving diagnostic accuracy in ultrasound imaging. The NBGL framework includes a noise-aware interaction weight generation (NIWG) module that estimates noise level via 3D Laplacian filtering and median absolute deviation, and a weighted feature-wise linear modulation (wFiLM) module for adaptive cross-branch modulation.

Traditional denoising methods often blur boundaries, while generative learning approaches (e.g., GANs, diffusion models) have shown promise but struggle with varying noise levels. Boundary enhancement techniques aim to preserve edge information critical for diagnosis.

Researchers propose APRTrack, a hierarchical perturbation and retrieval framework for RGB-Event visual object tracking that uses adversarial perturbations at modality and spatial levels to simulate real-world signal degradation, and introduces Footprint-guided Channel-calibrated Hopfield Retrieval (FCHR) for reliable historical information compensation. This work addresses the critical challenge of robust multi-modal tracking under signal degradation, which is essential for real-world applications like autonomous driving and surveillance where either RGB or event data may be unreliable. The proposed method achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks, advancing the practicality of event-based vision. APRTrack uses two adversarial perturbation branches: one for full-modal failure and another for localized target region absence, with a hierarchical routing mechanism to prevent feature collapse. The FCHR module calibrates the retrieval metric space based on association footprints before Hopfield matching, enabling controllable historical feature compensation bounded to target regions.

However, in harsh environments, either modality can degrade (e.g., motion blur in RGB or noise in events), and targets may be partially occluded. Adversarial perturbations are small, carefully crafted input changes that can fool neural networks; here they are used constructively to simulate degradation during training.

<a id="item-200"></a>
## [New Possibility Concept for Real-World Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02655) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new concept of possibility for real-world events is proposed, based on prerequisites and constraints, using Łukasiewicz logic, as an alternative to Zadeh's 1978 theory. This theory could improve planning and decision-making by quantifying which plans are most feasible, potentially impacting AI planning and human reasoning models. The possibility of an event is computed as a function of the probabilities that prerequisites hold and constraints do not. An illustrative example for vehicle route planning is provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Possibility theory, introduced by Lotfi Zadeh in 1978, deals with uncertainty and is based on fuzzy sets. Łukasiewicz logic is a many-valued logic that extends classical binary logic to handle degrees of truth, often used in fuzzy logic systems.

<a id="item-201"></a>
## [CLEF HIPE-2026: Person-Place Relation Extraction from Historical Texts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17663) ⭐️ 6.0/10

CLEF HIPE-2026 is a new evaluation lab that tasks participants with extracting person-place relations from noisy, multilingual historical texts, introducing a three-fold evaluation profile that jointly assesses accuracy, computational efficiency, and domain generalization. This lab addresses the challenging problem of semantic relation extraction in historical documents, which is crucial for digital humanities research such as knowledge-graph construction and historical biography reconstruction, while also promoting efficiency and generalization in NLP systems. Systems must classify relations as 'at' (ever been at this place) or 'isAt' (located at publication time), requiring reasoning over temporal and geographical cues across multiple languages and time periods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Relation extraction is a natural language processing task that identifies semantic relationships between entities in text. Historical texts are often noisy and multilingual, making extraction difficult. HIPE-2026 builds on prior HIPE campaigns (2020, 2022) that focused on entity recognition and linking, now extending to relation extraction.

<a id="item-202"></a>
## [DMSC: Dynamic Multi-Scale Coordination for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02753) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes DMSC, a dynamic multi-scale coordination framework that integrates an Elastic Multi-Scale Patch Decomposition block (EMPD), a Triad Interaction Block (TIB), and an Adaptive Scale Routing Mixture-of-Experts block (ASR-MoE) to improve time series forecasting. DMSC addresses key limitations of existing methods—static decomposition, fragmented dependency modeling, and inflexible fusion—achieving state-of-the-art performance on 13 benchmarks with superior computational efficiency. The EMPD dynamically segments sequences into hierarchical patches with exponentially scaled granularities, while TIB jointly models intra-patch, inter-patch, and cross-variable dependencies; ASR-MoE fuses multi-scale predictions using specialized global and local experts with temporal-aware weighting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting aims to predict future values based on historical data, but complex temporal dependencies across multiple scales pose challenges. Existing methods often use fixed decomposition strategies or single-scale modeling, limiting their ability to capture intricate patterns. DMSC introduces adaptive patch decomposition and dynamic routing to overcome these issues.

<a id="item-203"></a>
## [TLG: Heterogeneous Network Boosts Weakly-Supervised Few-Shot Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16159) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes TLG, a homologous but heterogeneous network with heterogeneous visual aggregation (HA) and transfer (HT) modules, plus CLIP-based textual information, achieving state-of-the-art results in weakly-supervised few-shot semantic segmentation (WFSS) with only 1/24 of the parameters of existing models. This work is significant because it is the first weakly-supervised (image-level) model to outperform fully supervised (pixel-level) models under the same backbone architectures, potentially reducing annotation costs while improving segmentation performance. TLG achieves a 13.2% improvement on Pascal-5^i and a 9.7% improvement on COCO-20^i over previous state-of-the-art WFSS methods. The code is available at https://github.com/jarch-ma/TLG.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 27, 04:00

**Background**: Few-shot semantic segmentation aims to segment novel object classes with only a few annotated examples. Weakly-supervised methods use weaker annotations (e.g., image-level labels) instead of pixel-level masks to reduce annotation effort. Meta-learning approaches typically use identical network architectures for support and query images, which can lead to over-semantic homogenization.

**Tags**: `#few-shot segmentation`, `#weakly-supervised learning`, `#meta-learning`, `#CLIP`, `#semantic segmentation`
